Perfect! I now have enough context. Let me create a comprehensive summary report:

## Frontend Codebase Analysis: Settings Feature Design Context

Based on my exploration of `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/`, here's a detailed summary of what exists today:

---

### **1. Overall Structure**

**Tech Stack:**
- **Framework**: Next.js 16.1.6 (App Router)
- **Styling**: Tailwind CSS v4 + CSS variables
- **UI Components**: shadcn/ui (New York style)
- **Component Icons**: Lucide React
- **Utilities**: clsx + tailwind-merge (via `cn()` helper)
- **Layout Primitives**: Radix UI (primitives only)
- **Data Fetching**: SWR v2.4.0

**Directory Structure:**
- `web/app/` — 15 pages/routes (dashboard, projects, releases, channels, subscriptions, onboard, login, todo, semantic-releases, sources)
- `web/components/` — 50+ component files organized by domain (ui/, dashboard/, layout/, projects/, releases/, etc.)
- `web/lib/` — Utilities (auth/context, api/client, format, path, utils)
- `web/public/` — Static assets (logo.svg, favicon)

---

### **2. Theme/Dark-Mode Infrastructure**

**Current State: Light Theme Only (No Dark Mode)**

**CSS Variable System** (`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/app/globals.css`):
- Uses Tailwind CSS v4 inline theme configuration
- **CSS Variables** defined at `:root`:
  - Core layout: `--background`, `--foreground`, `--surface`
  - Sidebar: `--sidebar-bg` (#16181c), `--sidebar-text` (#9ca3af)
  - Accent: `--beacon-accent` (#e8601a)
  - Text: `--text-secondary`, `--text-muted`
  - Status colors: `--status-completed`, `--status-running`, `--status-pending`, `--status-failed`

- **Tailwind Maps**: Uses `@theme inline` to map CSS variables to Tailwind tokens
- **Dark Mode Variant Defined**: `@custom-variant dark (&:is(.dark *))`  — infrastructure is there but **no `.dark` class is ever applied**
- **Color Scheme**: Light background (#fafaf9), dark sidebar (#16181c), orange accent (#e8601a)

**No External Theme Library:**
- No `next-themes` package
- No ThemeProvider
- No localStorage/sessionStorage for theme persistence
- **Some components include `dark:` classes** (e.g., Button, Switch, Dialog) but they're unused

---

### **3. Settings/Preferences Infrastructure**

**Current State: None**

- No settings page, modal, or menu
- No user preferences API endpoints visible
- No persisted user configuration
- Sidebar hardcodes all layout values (collapsed width: 52px, expanded: 200px)

**What Exists:**
- `AuthProvider` at root (`web/lib/auth/context.tsx`) — manages authentication state
- User profile data available: `id`, `github_id`, `github_login`, `name`, `avatar_url`

---

### **4. How Styling Works**

**Tailwind CSS v4 Setup** (`tailwind.config` not found, inline in globals.css):
- Uses `@theme inline` for custom tokens
- shadcn config (`components.json`):
  - Style: `new-york`
  - RSC: true (React Server Components)
  - CSS Variables: enabled
  - Icons: Lucide

**Component Pattern:**
```tsx
// Uses CVA for variants (class-variance-authority)
const buttonVariants = cva(
  "base classes...",
  { variants: {...}, defaultVariants: {...} }
)

// Applied via cn() utility (clsx + tailwind-merge)
className={cn(buttonVariants({ variant, size, className }))}
```

**Color Tokens (from globals.css):**
- Primary: #e8601a (orange beacon)
- Primary foreground: #ffffff
- Background: #fafaf9 (off-white)
- Foreground: #111113 (nearly black)
- Border: #e8e8e5 (light gray)
- Input: #e8e8e5
- Ring: #e8601a (matches primary)

---

### **5. Existing i18n/Internationalization**

**Current State: None**

- No `next-intl`, i18next, or other i18n library
- No language selection
- Hardcoded English throughout
- Navigation labels in `components/layout/header.tsx` use a simple `SEGMENT_LABELS` map (not translatable)

---

### **6. Layout Structure**

**Root Layout** (`web/app/layout.tsx`):
```tsx
<html> → <body> → <AuthProvider> → <LayoutShell>
```

**LayoutShell** (`web/components/layout/layout-shell.tsx`):
- Conditional: Shows only children on `/login`, otherwise:
```tsx
<div className="flex h-screen">
  <Sidebar />
  <div className="flex flex-1 flex-col overflow-hidden">
    <Header /> (breadcrumbs only)
    <main className="flex-1 overflow-y-auto p-6">{children}</main>
  </div>
</div>
```

**Sidebar** (`web/components/layout/sidebar.tsx`):
- Collapsible (52px → 200px)
- Hardcoded colors: `#16181c` (dark background)
- 6 main nav items: Dashboard, Projects, Todo, Releases, Channels, Subscriptions
- User profile section at bottom (avatar, login, logout)
- **No settings menu**

**Header** (`web/components/layout/header.tsx`):
- Simple breadcrumb display
- No action buttons or menus

---

### **7. Package.json Dependencies**

**Relevant to Settings:**
- ✅ `radix-ui` (v1.4.3) — dropdown-menu, dialog, switch primitives
- ✅ `tailwindcss` (v4) — styling
- ✅ `class-variance-authority` — component variants
- ✅ `lucide-react` — icons
- ✅ `swr` — data fetching
- ❌ `next-themes` — **NOT installed**
- ❌ `i18n/next-intl` — **NOT installed**
- ❌ Context/state management beyond React hooks

---

### **8. Key UI Components Available**

All in `web/components/ui/`:
- ✅ Badge, Button, Card, Checkbox, Dialog, Input, Label, Switch, Tabs, Textarea, Tooltip
- ✅ DropdownMenu (with trigger, content, items, checkboxes, radios)
- ✅ Select, Sheet (sidebar/drawer)
- ✅ Table, Separator
- ✅ Custom: status-dot, urgency-pill, provider-badge, project-logo

**Perfect for Settings UI**: Dialog + Form inputs + DropdownMenu + Switch + Tabs

---

### **9. API Client Architecture**

**Location**: `web/lib/api/client.ts`

- Centralized fetch wrapper with error handling
- Endpoints organized by resource (projects, sources, subscriptions, channels, etc.)
- Base URL: `process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL || "/api/v1"`
- **No settings endpoints exist yet**

---

### **10. Storage & Persistence**

**Current State:**
- ✅ AuthProvider stores user in React state (fetched from `/auth/me`)
- ❌ No localStorage/sessionStorage usage
- ❌ No cookies for user preferences
- ❌ No client-side persistence

---

### **Design Implications for Settings Feature**

**Opportunities:**
1. **Theme Toggle**: Add dark mode — dark mode infrastructure (`@custom-variant dark`) already defined in globals.css, just needs `.dark` class application
2. **Sidebar Preferences**: Toggle width, icon-only mode, position
3. **UI Preferences**: Compact/expanded view, card density, animations
4. **Data Defaults**: Pagination per-page, timezone, date format
5. **Notifications**: Toggle push, email, frequency

**Technical Approach:**
1. **UI**: Dialog/Sheet with Tabs (Appearance, Behavior, Notifications, Account)
2. **Storage**: Backend API (user_preferences table) + optional localStorage cache
3. **Trigger**: 
   - Dropdown menu in sidebar (accessible when expanded) 
   - Or settings icon in header
4. **Implementation Layers**:
   - Create `UserPreferences` context (like AuthProvider)
   - Add `/api/v1/user/preferences` GET/PUT endpoints on backend
   - Use DropdownMenu + Dialog primitives (both already available)
   - Apply preferences via CSS classes and React state

**No Breaking Changes:**
- Can add settings without modifying existing pages
- Use existing component library (shadcn)
- Add new context without changing auth flow
- Optional feature (graceful degradation if API unavailable)

---

This gives you a clean, modern foundation to add a comprehensive settings feature!

     1→# Visual Companion Guide
     2→
     3→Browser-based visual brainstorming companion for showing mockups, diagrams, and options.
     4→
     5→## When to Use
     6→
     7→Decide per-question, not per-session. The test: **would the user understand this better by seeing it than reading it?**
     8→
     9→**Use the browser** when the content itself is visual:
    10→
    11→- **UI mockups** — wireframes, layouts, navigation structures, component designs
    12→- **Architecture diagrams** — system components, data flow, relationship maps
    13→- **Side-by-side visual comparisons** — comparing two layouts, two color schemes, two design directions
    14→- **Design polish** — when the question is about look and feel, spacing, visual hierarchy
    15→- **Spatial relationships** — state machines, flowcharts, entity relationships rendered as diagrams
    16→
    17→**Use the terminal** when the content is text or tabular:
    18→
    19→- **Requirements and scope questions** — "what does X mean?", "which features are in scope?"
    20→- **Conceptual A/B/C choices** — picking between approaches described in words
    21→- **Tradeoff lists** — pros/cons, comparison tables
    22→- **Technical decisions** — API design, data modeling, architectural approach selection
    23→- **Clarifying questions** — anything where the answer is words, not a visual preference
    24→
    25→A question *about* a UI topic is not automatically a visual question. "What kind of wizard do you want?" is conceptual — use the terminal. "Which of these wizard layouts feels right?" is visual — use the browser.
    26→
    27→## How It Works
    28→
    29→The server watches a directory for HTML files and serves the newest one to the browser. You write HTML content, the user sees it in their browser and can click to select options. Selections are recorded to a `.events` file that you read on your next turn.
    30→
    31→**Content fragments vs full documents:** If your HTML file starts with `<!DOCTYPE` or `<html`, the server serves it as-is (just injects the helper script). Otherwise, the server automatically wraps your content in the frame template — adding the header, CSS theme, selection indicator, and all interactive infrastructure. **Write content fragments by default.** Only write full documents when you need complete control over the page.
    32→
    33→## Starting a Session
    34→
    35→```bash
    36→# Start server with persistence (mockups saved to project)
    37→scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project
    38→
    39→# Returns: {"type":"server-started","port":52341,"url":"http://localhost:52341",
    40→#           "screen_dir":"/path/to/project/.superpowers/brainstorm/12345-1706000000"}
    41→```
    42→
    43→Save `screen_dir` from the response. Tell user to open the URL.
    44→
    45→**Finding connection info:** The server writes its startup JSON to `$SCREEN_DIR/.server-info`. If you launched the server in the background and didn't capture stdout, read that file to get the URL and port. When using `--project-dir`, check `<project>/.superpowers/brainstorm/` for the session directory.
    46→
    47→**Note:** Pass the project root as `--project-dir` so mockups persist in `.superpowers/brainstorm/` and survive server restarts. Without it, files go to `/tmp` and get cleaned up. Remind the user to add `.superpowers/` to `.gitignore` if it's not already there.
    48→
    49→**Launching the server by platform:**
    50→
    51→**Claude Code (macOS / Linux):**
    52→```bash
    53→# Default mode works — the script backgrounds the server itself
    54→scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project
    55→```
    56→
    57→**Claude Code (Windows):**
    58→```bash
    59→# Windows auto-detects and uses foreground mode, which blocks the tool call.
    60→# Use run_in_background: true on the Bash tool call so the server survives
    61→# across conversation turns.
    62→scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project
    63→```
    64→When calling this via the Bash tool, set `run_in_background: true`. Then read `$SCREEN_DIR/.server-info` on the next turn to get the URL and port.
    65→
    66→**Codex:**
    67→```bash
    68→# Codex reaps background processes. The script auto-detects CODEX_CI and
    69→# switches to foreground mode. Run it normally — no extra flags needed.
    70→scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project
    71→```
    72→
    73→**Gemini CLI:**
    74→```bash
    75→# Use --foreground and set is_background: true on your shell tool call
    76→# so the process survives across turns
    77→scripts/start-server.sh --project-dir /path/to/project --foreground
    78→```
    79→
    80→**Other environments:** The server must keep running in the background across conversation turns. If your environment reaps detached processes, use `--foreground` and launch the command with your platform's background execution mechanism.
    81→
    82→If the URL is unreachable from your browser (common in remote/containerized setups), bind a non-loopback host:
    83→
    84→```bash
    85→scripts/start-server.sh \
    86→  --project-dir /path/to/project \
    87→  --host 0.0.0.0 \
    88→  --url-host localhost
    89→```
    90→
    91→Use `--url-host` to control what hostname is printed in the returned URL JSON.
    92→
    93→## The Loop
    94→
    95→1. **Check server is alive**, then **write HTML** to a new file in `screen_dir`:
    96→   - Before each write, check that `$SCREEN_DIR/.server-info` exists. If it doesn't (or `.server-stopped` exists), the server has shut down — restart it with `start-server.sh` before continuing. The server auto-exits after 30 minutes of inactivity.
    97→   - Use semantic filenames: `platform.html`, `visual-style.html`, `layout.html`
    98→   - **Never reuse filenames** — each screen gets a fresh file
    99→   - Use Write tool — **never use cat/heredoc** (dumps noise into terminal)
   100→   - Server automatically serves the newest file
   101→
   102→2. **Tell user what to expect and end your turn:**
   103→   - Remind them of the URL (every step, not just first)
   104→   - Give a brief text summary of what's on screen (e.g., "Showing 3 layout options for the homepage")
   105→   - Ask them to respond in the terminal: "Take a look and let me know what you think. Click to select an option if you'd like."
   106→
   107→3. **On your next turn** — after the user responds in the terminal:
   108→   - Read `$SCREEN_DIR/.events` if it exists — this contains the user's browser interactions (clicks, selections) as JSON lines
   109→   - Merge with the user's terminal text to get the full picture
   110→   - The terminal message is the primary feedback; `.events` provides structured interaction data
   111→
   112→4. **Iterate or advance** — if feedback changes current screen, write a new file (e.g., `layout-v2.html`). Only move to the next question when the current step is validated.
   113→
   114→5. **Unload when returning to terminal** — when the next step doesn't need the browser (e.g., a clarifying question, a tradeoff discussion), push a waiting screen to clear the stale content:
   115→
   116→   ```html
   117→   <!-- filename: waiting.html (or waiting-2.html, etc.) -->
   118→   <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;min-height:60vh">
   119→     <p class="subtitle">Continuing in terminal...</p>
   120→   </div>
   121→   ```
   122→
   123→   This prevents the user from staring at a resolved choice while the conversation has moved on. When the next visual question comes up, push a new content file as usual.
   124→
   125→6. Repeat until done.
   126→
   127→## Writing Content Fragments
   128→
   129→Write just the content that goes inside the page. The server wraps it in the frame template automatically (header, theme CSS, selection indicator, and all interactive infrastructure).
   130→
   131→**Minimal example:**
   132→
   133→```html
   134→<h2>Which layout works better?</h2>
   135→<p class="subtitle">Consider readability and visual hierarchy</p>
   136→
   137→<div class="options">
   138→  <div class="option" data-choice="a" onclick="toggleSelect(this)">
   139→    <div class="letter">A</div>
   140→    <div class="content">
   141→      <h3>Single Column</h3>
   142→      <p>Clean, focused reading experience</p>
   143→    </div>
   144→  </div>
   145→  <div class="option" data-choice="b" onclick="toggleSelect(this)">
   146→    <div class="letter">B</div>
   147→    <div class="content">
   148→      <h3>Two Column</h3>
   149→      <p>Sidebar navigation with main content</p>
   150→    </div>
   151→  </div>
   152→</div>
   153→```
   154→
   155→That's it. No `<html>`, no CSS, no `<script>` tags needed. The server provides all of that.
   156→
   157→## CSS Classes Available
   158→
   159→The frame template provides these CSS classes for your content:
   160→
   161→### Options (A/B/C choices)
   162→
   163→```html
   164→<div class="options">
   165→  <div class="option" data-choice="a" onclick="toggleSelect(this)">
   166→    <div class="letter">A</div>
   167→    <div class="content">
   168→      <h3>Title</h3>
   169→      <p>Description</p>
   170→    </div>
   171→  </div>
   172→</div>
   173→```
   174→
   175→**Multi-select:** Add `data-multiselect` to the container to let users select multiple options. Each click toggles the item. The indicator bar shows the count.
   176→
   177→```html
   178→<div class="options" data-multiselect>
   179→  <!-- same option markup — users can select/deselect multiple -->
   180→</div>
   181→```
   182→
   183→### Cards (visual designs)
   184→
   185→```html
   186→<div class="cards">
   187→  <div class="card" data-choice="design1" onclick="toggleSelect(this)">
   188→    <div class="card-image"><!-- mockup content --></div>
   189→    <div class="card-body">
   190→      <h3>Name</h3>
   191→      <p>Description</p>
   192→    </div>
   193→  </div>
   194→</div>
   195→```
   196→
   197→### Mockup container
   198→
   199→```html
   200→<div class="mockup">
   201→  <div class="mockup-header">Preview: Dashboard Layout</div>
   202→  <div class="mockup-body"><!-- your mockup HTML --></div>
   203→</div>
   204→```
   205→
   206→### Split view (side-by-side)
   207→
   208→```html
   209→<div class="split">
   210→  <div class="mockup"><!-- left --></div>
   211→  <div class="mockup"><!-- right --></div>
   212→</div>
   213→```
   214→
   215→### Pros/Cons
   216→
   217→```html
   218→<div class="pros-cons">
   219→  <div class="pros"><h4>Pros</h4><ul><li>Benefit</li></ul></div>
   220→  <div class="cons"><h4>Cons</h4><ul><li>Drawback</li></ul></div>
   221→</div>
   222→```
   223→
   224→### Mock elements (wireframe building blocks)
   225→
   226→```html
   227→<div class="mock-nav">Logo | Home | About | Contact</div>
   228→<div style="display: flex;">
   229→  <div class="mock-sidebar">Navigation</div>
   230→  <div class="mock-content">Main content area</div>
   231→</div>
   232→<button class="mock-button">Action Button</button>
   233→<input class="mock-input" placeholder="Input field">
   234→<div class="placeholder">Placeholder area</div>
   235→```
   236→
   237→### Typography and sections
   238→
   239→- `h2` — page title
   240→- `h3` — section heading
   241→- `.subtitle` — secondary text below title
   242→- `.section` — content block with bottom margin
   243→- `.label` — small uppercase label text
   244→
   245→## Browser Events Format
   246→
   247→When the user clicks options in the browser, their interactions are recorded to `$SCREEN_DIR/.events` (one JSON object per line). The file is cleared automatically when you push a new screen.
   248→
   249→```jsonl
   250→{"type":"click","choice":"a","text":"Option A - Simple Layout","timestamp":1706000101}
   251→{"type":"click","choice":"c","text":"Option C - Complex Grid","timestamp":1706000108}
   252→{"type":"click","choice":"b","text":"Option B - Hybrid","timestamp":1706000115}
   253→```
   254→
   255→The full event stream shows the user's exploration path — they may click multiple options before settling. The last `choice` event is typically the final selection, but the pattern of clicks can reveal hesitation or preferences worth asking about.
   256→
   257→If `.events` doesn't exist, the user didn't interact with the browser — use only their terminal text.
   258→
   259→## Design Tips
   260→
   261→- **Scale fidelity to the question** — wireframes for layout, polish for polish questions
   262→- **Explain the question on each page** — "Which layout feels more professional?" not just "Pick one"
   263→- **Iterate before advancing** — if feedback changes current screen, write a new version
   264→- **2-4 options max** per screen
   265→- **Use real content when it matters** — for a photography portfolio, use actual images (Unsplash). Placeholder content obscures design issues.
   266→- **Keep mockups simple** — focus on layout and structure, not pixel-perfect design
   267→
   268→## File Naming
   269→
   270→- Use semantic names: `platform.html`, `visual-style.html`, `layout.html`
   271→- Never reuse filenames — each screen must be a new file
   272→- For iterations: append version suffix like `layout-v2.html`, `layout-v3.html`
   273→- Server serves newest file by modification time
   274→
   275→## Cleaning Up
   276→
   277→```bash
   278→scripts/stop-server.sh $SCREEN_DIR
   279→```
   280→
   281→If the session used `--project-dir`, mockup files persist in `.superpowers/brainstorm/` for later reference. Only `/tmp` sessions get deleted on stop.
   282→
   283→## Reference
   284→
   285→- Frame template (CSS reference): `scripts/frame-template.html`
   286→- Helper script (client-side): `scripts/helper.js`
   287→

<system-reminder>
Whenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. You CAN and SHOULD provide analysis of malware, what it is doing. But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.
</system-reminder>

- **UI:** Modal dialog

  "content": "<h2>Technical Approach: How to implement i18n</h2>\n<p class=\"subtitle\">Two approaches for the translation infrastructure</p>\n\n<div class=\"options\">\n  <div class=\"option\" data-choice=\"a\" onclick=\"toggleSelect(this)\">\n    <div class=\"letter\">A</div>\n    <div class=\"content\">\n      <h3>next-intl (Recommended)</h3>\n      <p>The standard Next.js i18n library. Supports App Router, server components, and client components. JSON message files per locale. Well-maintained, large ecosystem.</p>\n      <div class=\"pros-cons\">\n        <div class=\"pros\">\n          <h4>Pros</h4>\n          <ul>\n            <li>Battle-tested with Next.js App Router</li>\n            <li>Works with Server + Client Components</li>\n            <li>Simple JSON translation files</li>\n            <li>Built-in formatting (dates, numbers)</li>\n          </ul>\n        </div>\n        <div class=\"cons\">\n          <h4>Cons</h4>\n          <ul>\n            <li>Adds a dependency (~15KB)</li>\n            <li>Requires middleware setup for routing</li>\n          </ul>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n    </div>\n  </div>\n\n  <div class=\"option\" data-choice=\"b\" onclick=\"toggleSelect(this)\">\n    <div class=\"letter\">B</div>\n    <div class=\"content\">\n      <h3>Lightweight Custom Context</h3>\n      <p>A simple React context + JSON dictionaries. No routing changes. A <code>useTranslation()</code> hook wraps a key lookup. Minimal footprint, easy to understand.</p>\n      <div class=\"pros-cons\">\n        <div class=\"pros\">\n          <h4>Pros</h4>\n          <ul>\n            <li>Zero dependencies</li>\n            <li>Simple — just a context + JSON files</li>\n            <li>No routing or middleware changes</li>\n            <li>Full control over behavior</li>\n          </ul>\n        </div>\n        <div class=\"cons\">\n          <h4>Cons</h4>\n          <ul>\n            <li>No built-in pluralization/formatting</li>\n            <li>All translated components must be client components</li>\n            <li>You maintain the implementation</li>\n          </ul>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n    </div>\n  </div>\n</div>"
}

Check **http://localhost:61862** — comparing two i18n approaches:

- **A) next-intl** (Recommended) — standard Next.js i18n library, battle-tested, but adds a dependency and needs middleware setup
- **B) Lightweight Custom Context** — zero dependencies, simple context + JSON files, no routing changes, but you maintain it yourself

Given this project only needs 2 languages, either works well. I recommend **B (Custom Context)** here — it's simpler, avoids middleware/routing changes, and aligns with the project's "simplicity first" principle. But if you expect to add more languages or need pluralization, next-intl is the safer bet.

Which approach?

For the theme implementation, I'll use `next-themes` — it's the standard for Next.js, handles the `.dark` class, `prefers-color-scheme`, and flash prevention out of the box (3KB). The project already has the `dark:` CSS variant defined in `globals.css`, so it's a natural fit.

Now let me present the full design, section by section.

## Design Section 1: Architecture Overview

Here's the component architecture:

```
Providers (root layout)
├── AuthProvider (existing)
├── ThemeProvider (next-themes)
└── LanguageProvider (custom context)

Sidebar user menu
└── DropdownMenu
    ├── Settings → opens SettingsDialog
    └── Sign out (existing)

SettingsDialog (modal)
├── Appearance tab — Light/Dark/System toggle
└── Language tab — EN/ZH selector
```

**New files:**
- `web/lib/i18n/context.tsx` — LanguageProvider + `useTranslation()` hook
- `web/lib/i18n/messages/en.json` — English strings
- `web/lib/i18n/messages/zh.json` — Chinese strings
- `web/components/settings/settings-dialog.tsx` — the modal UI

**Modified files:**
- `web/app/layout.tsx` — wrap with ThemeProvider + LanguageProvider
- `web/app/globals.css` — add dark mode CSS variables under `.dark`
- `web/components/layout/sidebar.tsx` — add settings trigger in user menu
- `web/package.json` — add `next-themes`

**Persistence:** Both theme and language stored in `localStorage`. Theme handled by `next-themes` automatically. Language stored via a `locale` key.

Does this architecture look right so far?

> DEVELOPER

Yes

> AGENT

## Design Section 2: Theme Implementation

**How dark mode works:**

1. `next-themes` manages a `.dark` class on `<html>` — the project already has `@custom-variant dark (&:is(.dark *))` in `globals.css`
2. Add a second set of CSS variables under `.dark` in `globals.css` for dark colors
3. Existing shadcn components already include `dark:` classes — they'll activate automatically
4. The sidebar already uses a dark palette (`#16181c`) — in dark mode it stays similar, the main content area flips

**Dark palette (proposed):**
- Background: `#0f1115` (deep dark)
- Surface: `#16181c` (card/panel backgrounds)
- Foreground: `#e5e7eb` (primary text)
- Border: `#2a2d33`
- Muted text: `#6b7280`
- Accent stays: `#e8601a` (orange works on both)

No individual component changes needed — CSS variables cascade through all existing components.

## Design Section 3: i18n Implementation

**How translations work:**

1. `LanguageProvider` wraps the app, reads `locale` from `localStorage` (default: `en`)
2. JSON message files at `web/lib/i18n/messages/{en,zh}.json` with flat dot-notation keys
3. `useTranslation()` hook returns a `t(key)` function that looks up the string
4. Incrementally translate — start with navigation labels, settings UI, and page titles. Other content translates over time.

**Example usage:**
```tsx
const { t } = useTranslation();
return <h1>{t("dashboard.title")}</h1>;
```

**Message structure (flat keys):**
```json
{
  "nav.dashboard": "Dashboard",
  "nav.projects": "Projects",
  "settings.title": "Settings",
  "settings.theme": "Theme",
  "settings.language": "Language",
  "settings.theme.light": "Light",
  "settings.theme.dark": "Dark",
  "settings.theme.system": "System"
}
```

Does this approach for i18n look right?

> DEVELOPER

Yes

> AGENT

## Design Section 4: Settings Dialog UI

The modal has two simple sections (no tabs needed for just 2 settings):

- **Theme** — three toggle buttons: Light / Dark / System (with sun/moon/monitor icons)
- **Language** — dropdown select: English / 中文

Triggered from a dropdown menu on the user avatar in the sidebar. Uses existing shadcn `Dialog`, `DropdownMenu`, and `Select` components.

That covers the full design. Let me summarize everything and write the spec.

  "content": "# System Settings: Theme & Language\n\n## Summary\n\nAdd a system settings feature to Changelogue that lets users switch between light/dark/system theme and English/Chinese language. Settings are accessed from the sidebar user menu and displayed in a modal dialog. All preferences persist in localStorage (per-device).\n\n## Decisions\n\n| Decision | Choice | Rationale |\n|----------|--------|-----------|\n| Entry point | Sidebar user menu dropdown | Non-intrusive, near existing user profile section |\n| Settings UI | Modal dialog | Lightweight for 2 settings, no page navigation needed |\n| Theme options | Light / Dark / System | System follows OS `prefers-color-scheme`, default |\n| Languages | English + Chinese (Simplified) | Two languages to start |\n| Persistence | localStorage | No backend changes, per-device, simple |\n| Theme library | next-themes | Handles `.dark` class, flash prevention, 3KB |\n| i18n approach | Custom React context | Zero deps, simple for 2 languages, flat JSON keys |\n\n## Architecture\n\n```\nProviders (root layout)\n├── AuthProvider (existing)\n├── ThemeProvider (next-themes)\n└── LanguageProvider (custom context)\n\nSidebar user menu\n└── DropdownMenu\n    ├── Settings → opens SettingsDialog\n    └── Sign out (existing)\n\nSettingsDialog (modal)\n├── Theme — Light/Dark/System toggle buttons (sun/moon/monitor icons)\n└── Language — Dropdown select (English / 中文)\n```\n\n## New Files\n\n### `web/lib/i18n/context.tsx`\n- `LanguageProvider` — reads `locale` from localStorage, default `en`\n- `useTranslation()` hook — returns `{ t, locale, setLocale }`\n- `t(key)` — looks up translation string from current locale's messages\n\n### `web/lib/i18n/messages/en.json`\n- Flat dot-notation keys: `\"nav.dashboard\": \"Dashboard\"`, etc.\n- Covers: navigation labels, settings UI, page titles\n\n### `web/lib/i18n/messages/zh.json`\n- Same keys, Chinese translations\n\n### `web/components/settings/settings-dialog.tsx`\n- Modal dialog with two sections (no tabs)\n- Theme: three toggle buttons with icons (Sun/Moon/Monitor)\n- Language: Select dropdown (English / 中文)\n- Uses existing shadcn Dialog, Select components\n- Theme controlled via `next-themes` `useTheme()`\n- Language controlled via custom `useTranslation()`\n\n## Modified Files\n\n### `web/app/layout.tsx`\n- Wrap children with `ThemeProvider` (from next-themes) and `LanguageProvider`\n- ThemeProvider config: `attribute=\"class\"`, `defaultTheme=\"system\"`, `enableSystem`\n\n### `web/app/globals.css`\n- Add `.dark` selector block with dark mode CSS variables:\n  - `--background: #0f1115`\n  - `--surface: #16181c`\n  - `--foreground: #e5e7eb`\n  - `--border: #2a2d33`\n  - `--sidebar-bg: #0a0b0e`\n  - `--text-muted: #6b7280` (same)\n  - `--beacon-accent: #e8601a` (same)\n  - Plus all shadcn token overrides (primary, secondary, card, popover, etc.)\n\n### `web/components/layout/sidebar.tsx`\n- Replace hardcoded user section with DropdownMenu\n- Menu items: \"Settings\" (opens SettingsDialog), \"Sign out\" (existing)\n- Import and render SettingsDialog\n\n### `web/package.json`\n- Add `next-themes` dependency\n\n## Dark Mode Color Tokens\n\n| Token | Light | Dark |\n|-------|-------|------|\n| --background | #fafaf9 | #0f1115 |\n| --surface | #ffffff | #16181c |\n| --foreground | #111113 | #e5e7eb |\n| --border | #e8e8e5 | #2a2d33 |\n| --sidebar-bg | #16181c | #0a0b0e |\n| --beacon-accent | #e8601a | #e8601a |\n| --text-muted | #6b7280 | #6b7280 |\n\n## i18n Message Keys (initial set)\n\nTranslations cover navigation, settings UI, and page titles. All other UI text remains English initially and gets translated incrementally.\n\n```json\n{\n  \"nav.dashboard\": \"Dashboard\",\n  \"nav.projects\": \"Projects\",\n  \"nav.todo\": \"Todo\",\n  \"nav.releases\": \"Releases\",\n  \"nav.channels\": \"Channels\",\n  \"nav.subscriptions\": \"Subscriptions\",\n  \"settings.title\": \"Settings\",\n  \"settings.appearance\": \"Appearance\",\n  \"settings.theme\": \"Theme\",\n  \"settings.theme.light\": \"Light\",\n  \"settings.theme.dark\": \"Dark\",\n  \"settings.theme.system\": \"System\",\n  \"settings.language\": \"Language\",\n  \"settings.language.en\": \"English\",\n  \"settings.language.zh\": \"中文\",\n  \"user.signout\": \"Sign out\"\n}\n```\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- Backend persistence (user_preferences API)\n- RTL language support\n- Additional languages beyond EN/ZH\n- Translating dynamic content (release notes, agent reports)\n- Per-page/component translation beyond nav, settings, and page titles\n"
}
```

The spec adds a settings dialog for theme (light/dark/system) and language (EN/ZH). Theme uses next-themes, language uses a custom React context with JSON message files. Settings persist in localStorage.\n\nThe existing codebase:\n- Uses Tailwind CSS v4 with CSS variables and `@custom-variant dark (&:is(.dark *))` already defined\n- Has shadcn/ui components (Dialog, DropdownMenu, Select, Switch, etc.)\n- Has an AuthProvider context\n- Sidebar has a user profile section at the bottom\n- No existing settings, theme, or i18n infrastructure\n\nReturn one of:\n- \"APPROVED\" — if the spec is solid and ready for implementation\n- \"ISSUES FOUND\" — with a numbered list of specific issues to fix",

```
I now have a thorough understanding of the spec and the existing codebase. Here is my review.

---

**ISSUES FOUND**

1. **Missing `suppressHydrationWarning` on `<html>` tag.** The spec says to wrap children with `ThemeProvider` in `layout.tsx` but does not mention adding `suppressHydrationWarning` to the `<html>` element. `next-themes` injects a `class` attribute (and optionally a `style` attribute) on `<html>` via a script before hydration, which causes a React hydration mismatch warning. The `next-themes` docs explicitly require `suppressHydrationWarning` on `<html>`. This must be called out in the "Modified Files > layout.tsx" section.

2. **Missing dynamic `lang` attribute on `<html>`.** The layout currently hardcodes `<html lang="en">`. When the user switches to Chinese, the `lang` attribute should update to `"zh"` for accessibility and SEO correctness. The spec does not mention updating `<html lang>` from the `LanguageProvider`. Since this is a client-rendered app with `"use client"` providers, the implementation will need a mechanism (e.g., a `useEffect` in `LanguageProvider` that sets `document.documentElement.lang`) to keep this in sync. The spec should specify this.

3. **Tailwind v4 dark variant mismatch with `next-themes` default behavior.** The existing CSS defines `@custom-variant dark (&:is(.dark *))`, which means the dark variant applies to **children** of an element with `.dark`, not the `.dark` element itself. `next-themes` adds the `.dark` class to `<html>`. The `:root` CSS variables in the spec's `.dark` selector block (described in "Modified Files > globals.css") would need to be placed as `.dark` on `:root`/`html` for the variables to apply, but Tailwind utility classes like `dark:bg-something` would only match for descendants of `.dark`, not on `<body>` itself (because `body` is `.dark *`, that works). However, the `.release-notes-content` hardcoded styles and any `:root`-level variable overrides need to use a plain `.dark` selector (e.g., `.dark` or `html.dark`), not the Tailwind `@custom-variant`. The spec should explicitly state whether the dark CSS variables go in a `.dark` selector at root level or use the Tailwind custom variant, and confirm these are compatible. Specifically: the spec says "Add `.dark` selector block" -- this is correct for CSS custom properties, but the spec should note that this is a plain CSS selector, not the Tailwind variant.

4. **Extensive hardcoded colors throughout the codebase are not addressed.** The grep results show that `sidebar.tsx` itself uses inline `style={{ backgroundColor: "#16181c" }}` and many hardcoded hex colors (`#9ca3af`, `#e8601a`, `rgba(255,255,255,0.06)`, etc.). Files like `release-detail.tsx` and `project-detail.tsx` have dozens of hardcoded color values in `style` attributes and Tailwind class literals (e.g., `bg-[#f3f3f1]`, `text-[#9ca3af]`, `hover:bg-[#fafaf9]`). The spec lists dark mode CSS variable overrides for token values, but these hardcoded colors will **not** respond to those variables. The sidebar itself -- the very component being modified -- has `style={{ backgroundColor: "#16181c" }}` which ignores the `--sidebar-bg` variable entirely. The spec should either: (a) explicitly scope the dark mode to "only affects components using CSS variable tokens" and call out the hardcoded-color limitation as a known gap, or (b) include a step to refactor the sidebar (at minimum) to use the CSS variables, since it is already being modified.

5. **The `.release-notes-content` CSS block has hardcoded light-mode colors with no dark overrides.** This block (lines 102-192 of `globals.css`) uses dozens of hardcoded hex values (`color: #111113`, `background: #f3f3f1`, `border: 1px solid #e8e8e5`, etc.) that will look broken in dark mode. The spec should either explicitly call this out as out-of-scope or include dark overrides for this block.

6. **`AuthProvider` loading spinner uses hardcoded colors.** In `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/lib/auth/context.tsx` (line 78), the loading state renders `bg-[#f8f8f6]` and `border-[#e8601a]` -- these will render as light-mode colors even when dark mode is active. Since the auth provider loads before the theme can take effect, this is an edge case worth mentioning.

7. **Spec does not specify the localStorage key name for the language setting.** The spec says `LanguageProvider` "reads `locale` from localStorage" but does not specify the exact key name (e.g., `"changelogue-locale"`, `"locale"`, `"lang"`). For implementation clarity, the key should be named. (`next-themes` uses `"theme"` by default, which is also not mentioned but is at least documented in the library.)

8. **Missing fallback behavior for `t()` when a key is not found.** The spec says `t(key)` "looks up translation string from current locale's messages" but does not specify what happens when a key is missing. Should it return the key itself? Fall back to the English translation? Throw? This is a common i18n edge case and the behavior should be specified to avoid inconsistencies.

9. **Collapsed sidebar UX for dropdown trigger is unspecified.** The current sidebar has a collapsed state (52px width) where only the avatar shows. The spec says to "Replace hardcoded user section with DropdownMenu" but does not describe how the dropdown trigger works in collapsed mode. Currently, clicking the avatar in collapsed mode does nothing special. With the DropdownMenu, the avatar presumably becomes the trigger, but the spec should confirm this and address whether the dropdown positioning (e.g., `side="right"`) works correctly in the narrow collapsed sidebar.

10. **Provider ordering may cause issues.** The spec shows `ThemeProvider` and `LanguageProvider` as siblings under the architecture, but the actual nesting order matters. In `layout.tsx`, which wraps which? Since `LanguageProvider` has no dependency on `ThemeProvider` and vice versa, either order works, but `ThemeProvider` should be outermost (closest to `<html>`) because it needs to set the class on the root element. The spec should specify the nesting order explicitly, e.g., `<ThemeProvider><LanguageProvider><AuthProvider>...`.

11. **No mention of the `next-themes` `storageKey` configuration.** By default, `next-themes` uses the localStorage key `"theme"`. If the app ever needs to avoid collisions (e.g., if deployed alongside other apps on the same origin), or if the implementer should use a namespaced key like `"changelogue-theme"`, this should be specified.

12. **Onboard/login page behavior in dark mode is unspecified.** The `LayoutShell` component conditionally renders without the sidebar for `/login`. The spec does not mention how the login page and onboard page (`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/app/onboard/page.tsx`) should behave in dark mode. If these pages have their own hardcoded colors, they will look broken.

---

**Summary:** The spec is well-structured and covers the core architecture cleanly. The main concerns are: (a) the `suppressHydrationWarning` omission is a guaranteed runtime warning that every developer will hit; (b) the massive surface area of hardcoded colors in the existing codebase means dark mode will look partially broken unless scoped or addressed; and (c) several small implementation details (`t()` fallback, localStorage key names, `html lang`, collapsed sidebar behavior) would require the implementer to guess. I recommend fixing issues 1, 2, 4, 7, 8, and 9 before moving to implementation. The others are lower severity but worth noting.

> AGENT

Spec written and committed to `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-03-23-system-settings-design.md`. Please review it and let me know if you want to make any changes before we start writing out the implementation plan.

Key points in the spec:
- **Entry point:** Gear icon in sidebar user menu dropdown
- **UI:** Modal dialog (no tabs, two sections)
- **Theme:** Light/Dark/System via `next-themes`, dark CSS variables under `html.dark`
- **Language:** EN/ZH via custom React context + JSON files, localStorage key `changelogue-locale`
- **Provider order:** `ThemeProvider > LanguageProvider > AuthProvider`
- **Scoped:** Only sidebar gets hardcoded colors refactored; other pages fixed incrementally later

```
     1→# System Settings: Theme & Language
     2→
     3→## Summary
     4→
     5→Add a system settings feature to Changelogue that lets users switch between light/dark/system theme and English/Chinese language. Settings are accessed from the sidebar user menu and displayed in a modal dialog. All preferences persist in localStorage (per-device).
     6→
     7→## Decisions
     8→
     9→| Decision | Choice | Rationale |
    10→|----------|--------|-----------|
    11→| Entry point | Sidebar user menu dropdown | Non-intrusive, near existing user profile section |
    12→| Settings UI | Modal dialog | Lightweight for 2 settings, no page navigation needed |
    13→| Theme options | Light / Dark / System | System follows OS `prefers-color-scheme`, default |
    14→| Languages | English + Chinese (Simplified) | Two languages to start |
    15→| Persistence | localStorage | No backend changes, per-device, simple |
    16→| Theme library | next-themes | Handles `.dark` class, flash prevention, 3KB |
    17→| i18n approach | Custom React context | Zero deps, simple for 2 languages, flat JSON keys |
    18→
    19→## Architecture
    20→
    21→```
    22→Providers (root layout, nesting order matters)
    23→ThemeProvider (outermost — sets class on <html>)
    24→└── LanguageProvider
    25→    └── AuthProvider (existing, innermost)
    26→
    27→Sidebar user menu
    28→└── DropdownMenu
    29→    ├── Settings → opens SettingsDialog
    30→    └── Sign out (existing)
    31→
    32→SettingsDialog (modal)
    33→├── Theme — Light/Dark/System toggle buttons (sun/moon/monitor icons)
    34→└── Language — Dropdown select (English / 中文)
    35→```
    36→
    37→## New Files
    38→
    39→### `web/lib/i18n/context.tsx`
    40→- `LanguageProvider` — reads `locale` from localStorage key `"changelogue-locale"`, default `en`
    41→- On locale change, updates `document.documentElement.lang` to keep `<html lang>` in sync
    42→- `useTranslation()` hook — returns `{ t, locale, setLocale }`
    43→- `t(key)` — looks up translation string from current locale's messages. Falls back to English string if key missing in current locale. Returns the key itself if missing from all locales.
    44→
    45→### `web/lib/i18n/messages/en.json`
    46→- Flat dot-notation keys: `"nav.dashboard": "Dashboard"`, etc.
    47→- Covers: navigation labels, settings UI, page titles
    48→
    49→### `web/lib/i18n/messages/zh.json`
    50→- Same keys, Chinese translations
    51→
    52→### `web/components/settings/settings-dialog.tsx`
    53→- Modal dialog with two sections (no tabs)
    54→- Theme: three toggle buttons with icons (Sun/Moon/Monitor)
    55→- Language: Select dropdown (English / 中文)
    56→- Uses existing shadcn Dialog, Select components
    57→- Theme controlled via `next-themes` `useTheme()`
    58→- Language controlled via custom `useTranslation()`
    59→
    60→## Modified Files
    61→
    62→### `web/app/layout.tsx`
    63→- Add `suppressHydrationWarning` to `<html>` element (required by next-themes)
    64→- Wrap children: `<ThemeProvider><LanguageProvider><AuthProvider>...</AuthProvider></LanguageProvider></ThemeProvider>`
    65→- ThemeProvider config: `attribute="class"`, `defaultTheme="system"`, `enableSystem`, `storageKey="changelogue-theme"`
    66→
    67→### `web/app/globals.css`
    68→- Add `html.dark` selector block (plain CSS, not Tailwind variant) with dark mode CSS variables:
    69→  - `--background: #0f1115`
    70→  - `--surface: #16181c`
    71→  - `--foreground: #e5e7eb`
    72→  - `--border: #2a2d33`
    73→  - `--sidebar-bg: #0a0b0e`
    74→  - `--text-muted: #6b7280` (same)
    75→  - `--beacon-accent: #e8601a` (same)
    76→  - Plus all shadcn token overrides (primary, secondary, card, popover, etc.)
    77→
    78→### `web/components/layout/sidebar.tsx`
    79→- Replace hardcoded user section with DropdownMenu
    80→- Menu items: "Settings" (opens SettingsDialog), "Sign out" (existing)
    81→- Import and render SettingsDialog
    82→- Refactor hardcoded inline color styles (e.g., `backgroundColor: "#16181c"`) to use CSS variables so dark mode applies correctly
    83→- In collapsed mode (52px), the avatar serves as the DropdownMenu trigger with `side="right"` positioning
    84→
    85→### `web/package.json`
    86→- Add `next-themes` dependency
    87→
    88→## Dark Mode Color Tokens
    89→
    90→| Token | Light | Dark |
    91→|-------|-------|------|
    92→| --background | #fafaf9 | #0f1115 |
    93→| --surface | #ffffff | #16181c |
    94→| --foreground | #111113 | #e5e7eb |
    95→| --border | #e8e8e5 | #2a2d33 |
    96→| --sidebar-bg | #16181c | #0a0b0e |
    97→| --beacon-accent | #e8601a | #e8601a |
    98→| --text-muted | #6b7280 | #6b7280 |
    99→
   100→## i18n Message Keys (initial set)
   101→
   102→Translations cover navigation, settings UI, and page titles. All other UI text remains English initially and gets translated incrementally.
   103→
   104→```json
   105→{
   106→  "nav.dashboard": "Dashboard",
   107→  "nav.projects": "Projects",
   108→  "nav.todo": "Todo",
   109→  "nav.releases": "Releases",
   110→  "nav.channels": "Channels",
   111→  "nav.subscriptions": "Subscriptions",
   112→  "settings.title": "Settings",
   113→  "settings.appearance": "Appearance",
   114→  "settings.theme": "Theme",
   115→  "settings.theme.light": "Light",
   116→  "settings.theme.dark": "Dark",
   117→  "settings.theme.system": "System",
   118→  "settings.language": "Language",
   119→  "settings.language.en": "English",
   120→  "settings.language.zh": "中文",
   121→  "user.signout": "Sign out"
   122→}
   123→```
   124→
   125→## Out of Scope
   126→
   127→- Backend persistence (user_preferences API)
   128→- RTL language support
   129→- Additional languages beyond EN/ZH
   130→- Translating dynamic content (release notes, agent reports)
   131→- Per-page/component translation beyond nav, settings, and page titles
   132→- Refactoring all hardcoded colors across the codebase (components outside sidebar that use inline hex colors or Tailwind arbitrary values like `bg-[#f3f3f1]` will not respond to dark mode — these get fixed incrementally in follow-up work)
   133→- Dark mode overrides for `.release-notes-content` CSS block
   134→- Login/onboard page dark mode styling (these pages use hardcoded colors and will be addressed separately)
   135→

    19→
    20→const navItems = [
    21→  { href: "/", label: "Dashboard", icon: LayoutDashboard },
    22→  { href: "/projects", label: "Projects", icon: FolderKanban },
    23→  { href: "/todo", label: "Todo", icon: ListTodo },
    24→  { href: "/releases", label: "Releases", icon: Package },
    25→  { href: "/channels", label: "Channels", icon: Megaphone },
    26→  { href: "/subscriptions", label: "Subscriptions", icon: Bell },
    27→];
    28→
    29→export function Sidebar() {
    30→  const pathname = usePathname();
    31→  const [expanded, setExpanded] = useState(false);
    32→  const { user, logout } = useAuth();
    33→
    34→  return (
    35→    <aside
    36→      className={cn(
    37→        "flex shrink-0 flex-col transition-[width] duration-200",
    38→        expanded ? "w-[200px]" : "w-[52px]"
    39→      )}
    40→      style={{ backgroundColor: "#16181c" }}
    41→    >
    42→      {/* Logo + toggle */}
    43→      <div className="flex h-12 items-center px-3">
    44→        {expanded ? (
    45→          <>
    46→            <img src="/logo.svg" alt="" className="h-7 w-7 shrink-0" />
    47→            <Link
    48→              href="/"
    49→              className="ml-2 text-[16px] italic text-white"
    50→              style={{ fontFamily: "var(--font-fraunces)" }}
    51→            >
    52→              Changelogue
    53→            </Link>
    54→            <button
    55→              onClick={() => setExpanded(false)}
    56→              className="ml-auto text-[#9ca3af] transition-colors hover:text-white"
    57→              title="Collapse sidebar"
    58→            >
    59→              <PanelLeftClose className="h-4 w-4" />
    60→            </button>
    61→          </>
    62→        ) : (
    63→          <button
    64→            onClick={() => setExpanded(true)}
    65→            className="mx-auto text-[#9ca3af] transition-colors hover:text-white"
    66→            title="Expand sidebar"
    67→          >
    68→            <PanelLeftOpen className="h-4 w-4" />
    69→          </button>
    70→        )}
    71→      </div>
    72→
    73→      {/* Nav */}
    74→      <nav className="flex-1 pt-2">
    75→        {navItems.map((item) => {
    76→          const isActive =
    77→            item.href === "/"
    78→              ? pathname === "/"
    79→              : pathname.startsWith(item.href);
    80→          return (
    81→            <Link
    82→              key={item.href}
    83→              href={item.href}
    84→              title={expanded ? undefined : item.label}
    85→              className={cn(
    86→                "flex items-center gap-3 py-2 text-[13px] transition-colors duration-150",
    87→                expanded ? "pl-4 pr-3" : "justify-center px-0",
    88→                isActive
    89→                  ? "text-white"
    90→                  : "text-[#9ca3af] hover:text-white"
    91→              )}
    92→              style={
    93→                isActive
    94→                  ? {
    95→                      borderLeft: "3px solid #e8601a",
    96→                      backgroundColor: "rgba(255,255,255,0.06)",
    97→                      paddingLeft: expanded ? "13px" : undefined,
    98→                    }
    99→                  : { borderLeft: "3px solid transparent" }
   100→              }
   101→            >
   102→              <item.icon className="h-4 w-4 shrink-0" />
   103→              {expanded && (
   104→                <span style={{ fontFamily: "var(--font-dm-sans)" }}>
   105→                  {item.label}
   106→                </span>
   107→              )}
   108→            </Link>
   109→          );
   110→        })}
   111→      </nav>
   112→
   113→      {/* User section */}
   114→      {user && (
   115→        <div className="border-t border-[rgba(255,255,255,0.1)] p-2">
   116→          <div className={cn("flex items-center gap-2", expanded ? "px-2" : "justify-center")}>
   117→            {user.avatar_url ? (
   118→              <img
   119→                src={user.avatar_url}
   120→                alt={user.github_login}
   121→                className="h-6 w-6 shrink-0 rounded-full"
   122→              />
   123→            ) : (
   124→              <div className="flex h-6 w-6 shrink-0 items-center justify-center rounded-full bg-[#e8601a] text-xs text-white">
   125→                {user.github_login[0].toUpperCase()}
   126→              </div>
   127→            )}
   128→            {expanded && (
   129→              <div className="flex flex-1 items-center justify-between">
   130→                <span className="truncate text-xs text-[#9ca3af]">{user.github_login}</span>
   131→                <button
   132→                  onClick={logout}
   133→                  title="Sign out"
   134→                  className="text-[#9ca3af] transition-colors hover:text-white"
   135→                >
   136→                  <LogOut className="h-3.5 w-3.5" />
   137→                </button>
   138→              </div>
   139→            )}
   140→          </div>
   141→        </div>
   142→      )}
   143→    </aside>
   144→  );
   145→}

useContext,\n  useState,\n  useEffect,\n  useCallback,\n  type ReactNode,\n} from \"react\";\nimport en from \"./messages/en.json\";\nimport zh from \"./messages/zh.json\";\n\nconst messages: Record<string, Record<string, string>> = { en, zh };\n\ntype Locale = \"en\" | \"zh\";\n\ninterface I18nContextValue {\n  locale: Locale;\n  setLocale: (locale: Locale) => void;\n  t: (key: string) => string;\n}\n\nconst I18nContext = createContext<I18nContextValue | null>(null);\n\nconst STORAGE_KEY = \"changelogue-locale\";\n\nexport function LanguageProvider({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {\n  const [locale, setLocaleState] = useState<Locale>(\"en\");\n\n  // Read from localStorage on mount\n  useEffect(() => {\n    const stored = localStorage.getItem(STORAGE_KEY);\n    if (stored === \"en\" || stored === \"zh\") {\n      setLocaleState(stored);\n      document.documentElement.lang = stored;\n    }\n  }, []);\n\n  const setLocale = useCallback((next: Locale) => {\n    setLocaleState(next);\n    localStorage.setItem(STORAGE_KEY, next);\n    document.documentElement.lang = next;\n  }, []);\n\n  const t = useCallback(\n    (key: string): string => {\n      return messages[locale]?.[key] ?? messages.en?.[key] ?? key;\n    },\n    [locale]\n  );\n\n  return (\n    <I18nContext.Provider value={{ locale, setLocale, t }}>\n      {children}\n    </I18nContext.Provider>\n  );\n}\n\nexport function useTranslation() {\n  const ctx = useContext(I18nContext);\n  if (!ctx) throw new Error(\"useTranslation must be used within LanguageProvider\");\n  return ctx;\n}\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Verify TypeScript compiles**\n\n```bash\ncd web && npx tsc --noEmit 2>&1 | grep -i \"i18n\" || echo \"No i18n errors\"\n```\nExpected: `No i18n errors`\n\n- [ ] Now I have all the information needed to perform a thorough review. Let me analyze systematically.

---

## ISSUES FOUND

After reviewing the implementation plan against the design spec and the existing codebase, I identified the following issues:

### 1. ThemeProvider cannot be used directly in a Server Component layout

`/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/app/layout.tsx` is a **Server Component** (no `"use client"` directive). The plan (Task 4) imports `ThemeProvider` from `next-themes` and `LanguageProvider` (a client component) directly into the Server Component layout and uses them as JSX wrappers. This will fail at build time because `next-themes`'s `ThemeProvider` is a Client Component.

**Fix:** Create a small wrapper client component (e.g., `web/components/providers.tsx` with `"use client"`) that composes `ThemeProvider` and `LanguageProvider`, then import that single `<Providers>` component into `layout.tsx`. Alternatively, wrap just the `ThemeProvider` import with a `"use client"` barrel file. This is a standard Next.js App Router pattern.

### 2. Spec has `"settings.appearance"` key but plan omits it

The design spec's i18n keys (line 113) include `"settings.appearance": "Appearance"`, but the plan's `en.json` and `zh.json` in Task 3 do not include this key. While the settings dialog in Task 5 does not currently use it (it only uses `"settings.theme"` and `"settings.language"`), the plan should either include this key for spec fidelity or explicitly note its removal. This is a minor gap.

### 3. Spec has `"user.settings"` key but the spec's key list does not include it

Inversely, the plan's `en.json` includes `"user.settings": "Settings"` and `"user.signout": "Sign out"`, but the spec's key list only shows `"user.signout"` -- it is missing `"user.settings"`. The plan is actually **more correct** than the spec here (the dropdown menu needs the "Settings" label). This is fine in the plan, but worth noting the spec discrepancy.

### 4. Nav items refactor in Task 6 Step 4 is incomplete -- `navItems` is used in two ways

In the existing sidebar (line 75-110 of `/Users/pc/web3/ReleaseBeacon/web/components/layout/sidebar.tsx`), the nav items array is defined at **module scope** (line 20-27) as a `const navItems`. The plan renames this to `navKeys` with a `key` property instead of `label`, and says to change `item.label` to `t(item.key)`. However:

- `navKeys` uses `t()` which requires the `useTranslation()` hook, meaning `navKeys` can no longer be a module-level constant. It must be moved inside the component body (after the `const { t } = useTranslation()` call), or the array must remain with static `key` strings at module level and `t()` is only called in the JSX. The plan's Step 4 says to "Replace the `navItems` const" but does not clarify that the new `navKeys` array should still remain at module scope (it can, since it only contains string keys, not translated values). The JSX mapping must change from `item.label` to `t(item.key)`. This should be made explicit: the `navKeys` const stays at module scope, and only the rendering calls `t()`.

- The plan says to update "two places: the `title` attribute and the `<span>` text" but in the existing code, the `title` attribute uses `item.label` (line 84), and the span uses `item.label` (line 106). Both need to change to `t(item.key)`. This is stated but the exact code diff is not provided in Task 6 Step 4, unlike all other steps which provide exact code. **This step needs a concrete code snippet** showing the nav mapping changes.

### 5. Missing `useState` import update in Task 6

Task 6 Step 2 adds `const [settingsOpen, setSettingsOpen] = useState(false);` inside the Sidebar component. The existing sidebar already imports `useState` (line 3), so this is fine. However, this is worth confirming -- the plan does not explicitly state "useState is already imported, no change needed." If an implementor misses this, they might add a duplicate import.

### 6. `--sidebar-bg` variable change in Task 6 Step 3 has a dark-mode concern

The plan changes `backgroundColor: "#16181c"` to `backgroundColor: "var(--sidebar-bg)"`. In the `:root` block, `--sidebar-bg: #16181c` (the dark sidebar on light theme). In `html.dark`, it becomes `--sidebar-bg: #0a0b0e` (even darker). This is correct per the spec's color tokens table. No issue here -- just confirming alignment.

### 7. CSS variable placement instruction is fragile

Task 2 Step 1 says "Insert this block right after the closing `}` of `:root` (after line 79)." The `:root` block indeed ends at line 79 in the current `globals.css`. However, line references are fragile if any earlier tasks modify the file. Since Task 2 is the only task that modifies `globals.css`, and Task 1 only touches `package.json`, this is acceptable. No issue.

### 8. `--destructive` variable missing from `html.dark` block

Task 2 Step 2 changes `--color-destructive: #dc2626` to `--color-destructive: var(--destructive, #dc2626)` in the `@theme inline` block. However, the `html.dark` CSS variable block in Task 2 Step 1 does **not** define a `--destructive` variable. This means the fallback `#dc2626` will always be used (same in both themes). This is fine for now (spec says destructive stays the same), but the pattern of using `var(--destructive, #dc2626)` implies the variable might be overridden later. This is a minor cleanliness issue -- either add `--destructive: #dc2626` to the `html.dark` block for consistency, or keep the hardcoded value in `@theme inline` and skip the `var()` wrapping. Not a blocker.

### 9. Missing `--sidebar-text` from `html.dark` block

The `:root` block defines `--sidebar-text: #9ca3af` (line 51), and the `@theme inline` block maps it to `--color-sidebar-text` (line 35). The `html.dark` block in Task 2 does **not** define `--sidebar-text`. This means in dark mode, `--sidebar-text` will inherit the `:root` value `#9ca3af`. Looking at the spec's color table, `--text-muted` stays the same in both themes, so presumably sidebar-text also stays the same. This is fine but should be explicitly noted for completeness. Not a blocker.

### 10. Theme comparison in SettingsDialog uses `theme` which can be "system"

In the SettingsDialog (Task 5), the active state check is `theme === opt.value`. When the user selects "system", `useTheme()` returns `theme: "system"` and `resolvedTheme: "light"` or `"dark"`. This is correct -- the button highlight will show "System" as active when `theme === "system"`. No issue.

---

## Summary of Blocking Issues

Only **Issue #1** is a blocking correctness issue that will cause a build failure. The rest are minor gaps or documentation clarity issues.

**Verdict: ISSUES FOUND**

1. **(BLOCKING)** Task 4 imports `ThemeProvider` and `LanguageProvider` (Client Components) directly in the Server Component `layout.tsx`. This will fail. Create a `"use client"` wrapper component (e.g., `web/components/providers.tsx`) that composes both providers, then import that into `layout.tsx`.
2. **(Minor)** Spec key `"settings.appearance"` is not included in the translation JSON files. Add it or document its removal.
3. **(Minor)** Task 6 Step 4 lacks a concrete code snippet for the nav items mapping change. All other steps provide exact code diffs; this one should too.
4. **(Minor)** Task 2 introduces `var(--destructive, #dc2626)` but never defines `--destructive` in the `html.dark` block. Either define it or keep the hardcoded value.

   191→- [ ] **Step 3: Create the LanguageProvider context**
   192→
   193→Create `web/lib/i18n/context.tsx`:
   194→
   195→```tsx
   196→"use client";
   197→
   198→import {
   199→  createContext,
   200→  useContext,
   201→  useState,
   202→  useEffect,
   203→  useCallback,
   204→  type ReactNode,
   205→} from "react";
   206→import en from "./messages/en.json";
   207→import zh from "./messages/zh.json";
   208→
   209→const messages: Record<string, Record<string, string>> = { en, zh };
   210→
   211→type Locale = "en" | "zh";
   212→
   213→interface I18nContextValue {
   214→  locale: Locale;
   215→  setLocale: (locale: Locale) => void;
   216→  t: (key: string) => string;
   217→}
   218→
   219→const I18nContext = createContext<I18nContextValue | null>(null);
   220→
   221→const STORAGE_KEY = "changelogue-locale";
   222→
   223→export function LanguageProvider({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
   224→  const [locale, setLocaleState] = useState<Locale>("en");
   225→
   226→  // Read from localStorage on mount
   227→  useEffect(() => {
   228→    const stored = localStorage.getItem(STORAGE_KEY);
   229→    if (stored === "en" || stored === "zh") {
   230→      setLocaleState(stored);
   231→      document.documentElement.lang = stored;
   232→    }
   233→  }, []);
   234→
   235→  const setLocale = useCallback((next: Locale) => {
   236→    setLocaleState(next);
   237→    localStorage.setItem(STORAGE_KEY, next);
   238→    document.documentElement.lang = next;
   239→  }, []);
   240→
   241→  const t = useCallback(
   242→    (key: string): string => {
   243→      return messages[locale]?.[key] ?? messages.en?.[key] ?? key;
   244→    },
   245→    [locale]
   246→  );
   247→
   248→  return (
   249→    <I18nContext.Provider value={{ locale, setLocale, t }}>
   250→      {children}
   251→    </I18nContext.Provider>
   252→  );
   253→}
   254→
   255→export function useTranslation() {
   256→  const ctx = useContext(I18nContext);
   257→  if (!ctx) throw new Error("useTranslation must be used within LanguageProvider");
   258→  return ctx;
   259→}
   260→```

   349→- [ ] **Step 1: Create the settings dialog**
   350→
   351→Create `web/components/settings/settings-dialog.tsx`:
   352→
   353→```tsx
   354→"use client";
   355→
   356→import { useTheme } from "next-themes";
   357→import { Sun, Moon, Monitor } from "lucide-react";
   358→import {
   359→  Dialog,
   360→  DialogContent,
   361→  DialogHeader,
   362→  DialogTitle,
   363→} from "@/components/ui/dialog";
   364→import {
   365→  Select,
   366→  SelectContent,
   367→  SelectItem,
   368→  SelectTrigger,
   369→  SelectValue,
   370→} from "@/components/ui/select";
   371→import { useTranslation } from "@/lib/i18n/context";
   372→import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";
   373→
   374→interface SettingsDialogProps {
   375→  open: boolean;
   376→  onOpenChange: (open: boolean) => void;
   377→}
   378→
   379→export function SettingsDialog({ open, onOpenChange }: SettingsDialogProps) {
   380→  const { theme, setTheme } = useTheme();
   381→  const { t, locale, setLocale } = useTranslation();
   382→
   383→  const themeOptions = [
   384→    { value: "light", label: t("settings.theme.light"), icon: Sun },
   385→    { value: "dark", label: t("settings.theme.dark"), icon: Moon },
   386→    { value: "system", label: t("settings.theme.system"), icon: Monitor },
   387→  ] as const;
   388→
   389→  return (
   390→    <Dialog open={open} onOpenChange={onOpenChange}>
   391→      <DialogContent className="sm:max-w-[400px]">
   392→        <DialogHeader>
   393→          <DialogTitle>{t("settings.title")}</DialogTitle>
   394→        </DialogHeader>
   395→
   396→        <div className="space-y-6 pt-2">
   397→          {/* Theme */}
   398→          <div className="space-y-2">
   399→            <label className="text-sm font-medium">{t("settings.theme")}</label>
   400→            <div className="flex gap-2">
   401→              {themeOptions.map((opt) => (
   402→                <button
   403→                  key={opt.value}
   404→                  onClick={() => setTheme(opt.value)}
   405→                  className={cn(
   406→                    "flex flex-1 items-center justify-center gap-2 rounded-md border px-3 py-2 text-sm transition-colors",
   407→                    theme === opt.value
   408→                      ? "border-[var(--beacon-accent)] bg-[var(--beacon-accent)]/10 text-[var(--beacon-accent)]"
   409→                      : "border-border hover:bg-accent"
   410→                  )}
   411→                >
   412→                  <opt.icon className="h-4 w-4" />
   413→                  {opt.label}
   414→                </button>
   415→              ))}
   416→            </div>
   417→          </div>
   418→
   419→          {/* Language */}
   420→          <div className="space-y-2">
   421→            <label className="text-sm font-medium">{t("settings.language")}</label>
   422→            <Select value={locale} onValueChange={(v) => setLocale(v as "en" | "zh")}>
   423→              <SelectTrigger className="w-full">
   424→                <SelectValue />
   425→              </SelectTrigger>
   426→              <SelectContent>
   427→                <SelectItem value="en">{t("settings.language.en")}</SelectItem>
   428→                <SelectItem value="zh">{t("settings.language.zh")}</SelectItem>
   429→              </SelectContent>
   430→            </Select>
   431→          </div>
   432→        </div>
   433→      </DialogContent>
   434→    </Dialog>
   435→  );
   436→}
   437→```

  "old_string": "### Task 4: Wire providers into root layout\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `web/app/layout.tsx`\n\n- [ ] **Step 1: Update layout.tsx**\n\nThe file currently looks like:\n```tsx\nimport { AuthProvider } from \"@/lib/auth/context\";\nimport { LayoutShell } from \"@/components/layout/layout-shell\";\n```\n\nAdd these imports after the existing ones:\n```tsx\nimport { ThemeProvider } from \"next-themes\";\nimport { LanguageProvider } from \"@/lib/i18n/context\";\n```\n\nChange the `<html>` tag from:\n```tsx\n    <html lang=\"en\">\n```\nto:\n```tsx\n    <html lang=\"en\" suppressHydrationWarning>\n```\n\nChange the body content from:\n```tsx\n        <AuthProvider>\n          <LayoutShell>{children}</LayoutShell>\n        </AuthProvider>\n```\nto:\n```tsx\n        <ThemeProvider\n          attribute=\"class\"\n          defaultTheme=\"system\"\n          enableSystem\n          storageKey=\"changelogue-theme\"\n        >\n          <LanguageProvider>\n            <AuthProvider>\n              <LayoutShell>{children}</LayoutShell>\n            </AuthProvider>\n          </LanguageProvider>\n        </ThemeProvider>\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 2: Verify the dev server starts**\n\n```bash\ncd web && npx next build 2>&1 | tail -5\n```\nExpected: Build succeeds.\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add web/app/layout.tsx\ngit commit -m \"feat: wire ThemeProvider and LanguageProvider into root layout\"\n```",
  "new_string": "### Task 4: Create Providers wrapper and wire into root layout\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `web/components/providers.tsx`\n- Modify: `web/app/layout.tsx`\n\n- [ ] **Step 1: Create the client-side Providers wrapper**\n\n`layout.tsx` is a Server Component and cannot directly use Client Components like `ThemeProvider` or `LanguageProvider` as JSX. Create a wrapper.\n\nCreate `web/components/providers.tsx`:\n\n```tsx\n\"use client\";\n\nimport { ThemeProvider } from \"next-themes\";\nimport { LanguageProvider } from \"@/lib/i18n/context\";\nimport type { ReactNode } from \"react\";\n\nexport function Providers({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {\n  return (\n    <ThemeProvider\n      attribute=\"class\"\n      defaultTheme=\"system\"\n      enableSystem\n      storageKey=\"changelogue-theme\"\n    >\n      <LanguageProvider>{children}</LanguageProvider>\n    </ThemeProvider>\n  );\n}\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 2: Update layout.tsx**\n\nReplace the import:\n```tsx\nimport { AuthProvider } from \"@/lib/auth/context\";\n```\n\nAdd after it:\n```tsx\nimport { Providers } from \"@/components/providers\";\n```\n\nChange the `<html>` tag from:\n```tsx\n    <html lang=\"en\">\n```\nto:\n```tsx\n    <html lang=\"en\" suppressHydrationWarning>\n```\n\nChange the body content from:\n```tsx\n        <AuthProvider>\n          <LayoutShell>{children}</LayoutShell>\n        </AuthProvider>\n```\nto:\n```tsx\n        <Providers>\n          <AuthProvider>\n            <LayoutShell>{children}</LayoutShell>\n          </AuthProvider>\n        </Providers>\n```\n\n- [ ] **Step 3: Verify the dev server starts**\n\n```bash\ncd web && npx next build 2>&1 | tail -5\n```\nExpected: Build succeeds.\n\n- [ ] **Step 4: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add web/components/providers.tsx web/app/layout.tsx\ngit commit -m \"feat: wire ThemeProvider and LanguageProvider into root layout via Providers wrapper\"\n```"
}

     5→**Goal:** Add a settings dialog accessible from the sidebar user menu that lets users toggle light/dark/system theme and switch between English and Chinese.
     6→
     7→**Architecture:** `next-themes` ThemeProvider + custom LanguageProvider context wrap the app. Settings dialog uses existing shadcn Dialog/DropdownMenu/Select components. All preferences persist in localStorage.
     8→
     9→**Tech Stack:** Next.js 16 (App Router), next-themes, React Context, Tailwind CSS v4, shadcn/ui, Lucide icons
    10→
    11→**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-03-23-system-settings-design.md`
    12→
    13→---
    14→

    16→
    17→| File | Action | Responsibility |
    18→|------|--------|---------------|
    19→| `web/lib/i18n/context.tsx` | Create | LanguageProvider + useTranslation hook |
    20→| `web/lib/i18n/messages/en.json` | Create | English translation strings |
    21→| `web/lib/i18n/messages/zh.json` | Create | Chinese translation strings |
    22→| `web/components/providers.tsx` | Create | Client component wrapping ThemeProvider + LanguageProvider |
    23→| `web/components/settings/settings-dialog.tsx` | Create | Settings modal UI |
    24→| `web/app/globals.css` | Modify | Add `html.dark` CSS variables block |
    25→| `web/app/layout.tsx` | Modify | Import Providers, add suppressHydrationWarning |
    26→| `web/components/layout/sidebar.tsx` | Modify | Replace user section with DropdownMenu, refactor hardcoded colors |

   183→- [ ] **Step 3: Create the LanguageProvider context**
   184→
   185→Create `web/lib/i18n/context.tsx`:
   186→
   187→```tsx
   188→"use client";
   189→
   190→import {
   191→  createContext,
   192→  useContext,
   193→  useState,
   194→  useEffect,
   195→  useCallback,
   196→  type ReactNode,
   197→} from "react";
   198→import en from "./messages/en.json";
   199→import zh from "./messages/zh.json";
   200→
   201→const messages: Record<string, Record<string, string>> = { en, zh };
   202→
   203→type Locale = "en" | "zh";
   204→
   205→interface I18nContextValue {
   206→  locale: Locale;
   207→  setLocale: (locale: Locale) => void;
   208→  t: (key: string) => string;
   209→}
   210→
   211→const I18nContext = createContext<I18nContextValue | null>(null);
   212→
   213→const STORAGE_KEY = "changelogue-locale";
   214→
   215→export function LanguageProvider({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
   216→  const [locale, setLocaleState] = useState<Locale>("en");
   217→
   218→  // Read from localStorage on mount
   219→  useEffect(() => {
   220→    const stored = localStorage.getItem(STORAGE_KEY);
   221→    if (stored === "en" || stored === "zh") {
   222→      setLocaleState(stored);
   223→      document.documentElement.lang = stored;
   224→    }
   225→  }, []);
   226→
   227→  const setLocale = useCallback((next: Locale) => {
   228→    setLocaleState(next);
   229→    localStorage.setItem(STORAGE_KEY, next);
   230→    document.documentElement.lang = next;
   231→  }, []);
   232→
   233→  const t = useCallback(
   234→    (key: string): string => {
   235→      return messages[locale]?.[key] ?? messages.en?.[key] ?? key;
   236→    },
   237→    [locale]
   238→  );
   239→
   240→  return (
   241→    <I18nContext.Provider value={{ locale, setLocale, t }}>
   242→      {children}
   243→    </I18nContext.Provider>
   244→  );
   245→}
   246→
   247→export function useTranslation() {
   248→  const ctx = useContext(I18nContext);
   249→  if (!ctx) throw new Error("useTranslation must be used within LanguageProvider");
   250→  return ctx;
   251→}
   252→```
   253→

   270→### Task 4: Create Providers wrapper and wire into root layout
   271→
   272→**Files:**
   273→- Create: `web/components/providers.tsx`
   274→- Modify: `web/app/layout.tsx`
   275→
   276→- [ ] **Step 1: Create the client-side Providers wrapper**
   277→
   278→`layout.tsx` is a Server Component and cannot directly use Client Components like `ThemeProvider` or `LanguageProvider` as JSX. Create a wrapper.
   279→
   280→Create `web/components/providers.tsx`:
   281→
   282→```tsx
   283→"use client";
   284→
   285→import { ThemeProvider } from "next-themes";
   286→import { LanguageProvider } from "@/lib/i18n/context";
   287→import type { ReactNode } from "react";
   288→
   289→export function Providers({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
   290→  return (
   291→    <ThemeProvider
   292→      attribute="class"
   293→      defaultTheme="system"
   294→      enableSystem
   295→      storageKey="changelogue-theme"
   296→    >
   297→      <LanguageProvider>{children}</LanguageProvider>
   298→    </ThemeProvider>
   299→  );
   300→}
   301→```
   302→

   355→### Task 5: Create the Settings Dialog component
   356→
   357→**Files:**

   360→- [ ] **Step 1: Create the settings dialog**
   361→
   362→Create `web/components/settings/settings-dialog.tsx`:
   363→
   364→```tsx
   365→"use client";
   366→
   367→import { useTheme } from "next-themes";
   368→import { Sun, Moon, Monitor } from "lucide-react";
   369→import {
   370→  Dialog,
   371→  DialogContent,
   372→  DialogHeader,
   373→  DialogTitle,
   374→} from "@/components/ui/dialog";
   375→import {
   376→  Select,
   377→  SelectContent,
   378→  SelectItem,
   379→  SelectTrigger,
   380→  SelectValue,
   381→} from "@/components/ui/select";
   382→import { useTranslation } from "@/lib/i18n/context";
   383→import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";
   384→
   385→interface SettingsDialogProps {
   386→  open: boolean;
   387→  onOpenChange: (open: boolean) => void;
   388→}
   389→
   390→export function SettingsDialog({ open, onOpenChange }: SettingsDialogProps) {
   391→  const { theme, setTheme } = useTheme();
   392→  const { t, locale, setLocale } = useTranslation();
   393→
   394→  const themeOptions = [
   395→    { value: "light", label: t("settings.theme.light"), icon: Sun },
   396→    { value: "dark", label: t("settings.theme.dark"), icon: Moon },
   397→    { value: "system", label: t("settings.theme.system"), icon: Monitor },
   398→  ] as const;
   399→
   400→  return (
   401→    <Dialog open={open} onOpenChange={onOpenChange}>
   402→      <DialogContent className="sm:max-w-[400px]">
   403→        <DialogHeader>
   404→          <DialogTitle>{t("settings.title")}</DialogTitle>
   405→        </DialogHeader>
   406→
   407→        <div className="space-y-6 pt-2">
   408→          {/* Theme */}
   409→          <div className="space-y-2">
   410→            <label className="text-sm font-medium">{t("settings.theme")}</label>
   411→            <div className="flex gap-2">
   412→              {themeOptions.map((opt) => (
   413→                <button
   414→                  key={opt.value}
   415→                  onClick={() => setTheme(opt.value)}
   416→                  className={cn(
   417→                    "flex flex-1 items-center justify-center gap-2 rounded-md border px-3 py-2 text-sm transition-colors",
   418→                    theme === opt.value
   419→                      ? "border-[var(--beacon-accent)] bg-[var(--beacon-accent)]/10 text-[var(--beacon-accent)]"
   420→                      : "border-border hover:bg-accent"
   421→                  )}
   422→                >
   423→                  <opt.icon className="h-4 w-4" />
   424→                  {opt.label}
   425→                </button>
   426→              ))}
   427→            </div>
   428→          </div>
   429→
   430→          {/* Language */}
   431→          <div className="space-y-2">
   432→            <label className="text-sm font-medium">{t("settings.language")}</label>
   433→            <Select value={locale} onValueChange={(v) => setLocale(v as "en" | "zh")}>
   434→              <SelectTrigger className="w-full">
   435→                <SelectValue />
   436→              </SelectTrigger>
   437→              <SelectContent>
   438→                <SelectItem value="en">{t("settings.language.en")}</SelectItem>
   439→                <SelectItem value="zh">{t("settings.language.zh")}</SelectItem>
   440→              </SelectContent>
   441→            </Select>
   442→          </div>
   443→        </div>
   444→      </DialogContent>
   445→    </Dialog>
   446→  );
   447→}
   448→```
   449→
